# Commands · M · part 4

1,391 entries, minutes-graph to mpt-send-to-review. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **minutes-graph**: Policy-safe relationship rankings, commitments, aliases, person profiles, and topic research. Always use Minutes' bounded native CLI surfaces; never build or read a durable graph cache. ([silverstein/minutes](https://github.com/silverstein/minutes/blob/HEAD/.opencode/commands/minutes-graph.md) · ★1.5k · 2 copies)
- **minutes-graph**: Cross-meeting entity graph — query who/what/when across all your meetings as structured data, with co-occurrence and cross-entity queries that text search can't answer. Use whenever the user says "show me everyone who mentioned X", "all mentions of Y across meetings", "who knows about Z", "graph", "across all meetings", "entity search", "first time we talked about", "trend for X over time", "who's been mentioned alongside", or wants to query meetings as an index rather than full-text search. Builds a JSON entity index on first run (one-time slow), then answers queries instantly. Surface this… ([jacob-split/minutes](https://github.com/jacob-split/minutes/blob/HEAD/.opencode/commands/minutes-graph.md))
- **minutes-ideas**: Surface recent voice memos and ideas captured from any device. Use when the user asks "what ideas did I have?", "what were my recent memos?", "what did I record while walking?", or wants to recall a captured thought. ([silverstein/minutes](https://github.com/silverstein/minutes/blob/HEAD/.opencode/commands/minutes-ideas.md) · ★1.5k · 3 copies)
- **minutes-ingest**: Extract facts from meetings and update your knowledge base — person profiles, chronological log, and index. Use when the user asks "ingest my meetings", "update my knowledge base", "extract facts from meetings", "sync meetings to wiki", "backfill knowledge", or wants their PARA/Obsidian/wiki profiles updated from conversation data. ([silverstein/minutes](https://github.com/silverstein/minutes/blob/HEAD/.opencode/commands/minutes-ingest.md) · ★1.5k · 3 copies)
- **minutes-lint**: Health-check your meeting knowledge for contradictions, stale commitments, and decision conflicts. Use when the user asks "any conflicts in my meetings", "check for stale action items", "lint my meetings", "consistency check", "are there contradictions", or wants to audit their decision history. ([silverstein/minutes](https://github.com/silverstein/minutes/blob/HEAD/.opencode/commands/minutes-lint.md) · ★1.5k · 3 copies)
- **minutes-list**: List recent meetings and voice memos. Use when the user asks "what meetings did I have", "show my recent recordings", "any meetings today", "list my voice memos", or wants an overview of their meeting history. Also use when they need to find a specific meeting by browsing rather than searching. ([silverstein/minutes](https://github.com/silverstein/minutes/blob/HEAD/.opencode/commands/minutes-list.md) · ★1.5k · 3 copies)
- **minutes-live-sidekick**: Act as the user's live meeting sidekick inside the current terminal agent session. Use when the user explicitly asks you, the terminal agent, to watch a meeting, follow the live transcript, answer during the call, offer strategist thoughts, silently watch for risks, or track decisions. Do not use this skill to start or control the separate Minutes Coach HUD; explicit Coach or HUD lifecycle requests belong to minutes-copilot, and an ambiguous request such as "coach me live" requires one short surface clarification. ([silverstein/minutes](https://github.com/silverstein/minutes/blob/HEAD/.opencode/commands/minutes-live-sidekick.md) · ★1.5k · 2 copies)

- **minutes-mirror**: Self-coaching analysis of your own behavior across meetings — talk-time ratio, filler words, hedging language, monologue length, energy patterns, and (when meetings are tagged via /minutes-tag) what your behavior in winning meetings looks like vs losing ones. Use this whenever the user says "how did I do", "review my last meeting", "mirror", "self-review", "show my patterns", "coach me", "where am I weak", "talk time", "am I improving", "what do I do in meetings I win", "feedback on me", or asks for any kind of personal feedback on their own meeting behavior. - **minutes-note**: Add a note to the current recording or annotate a past meeting. Plain text input — no markdown needed. ([silverstein/minutes](https://github.com/silverstein/minutes/blob/HEAD/.opencode/commands/minutes-note.md) · ★1.5k · 3 copies)

- **minutes-record**: Start or stop recording a meeting, call, or voice memo. Use this whenever the user says "record", "start recording", "capture this meeting", "stop recording", "I'm in a meeting", "take notes on this call", or wants to transcribe live audio. Also use when they ask about recording status or want to know if something is being recorded. - **minutes-search**: Search past meeting transcripts and voice memos for specific topics, people, decisions, or ideas. Use this whenever the user asks "what did we discuss about X", "find that meeting where we talked about Y", "what did Alex say", "did we decide on", "what was that idea about", or any question that could be answered by searching their meeting history. - **minutes-setup**: Guided first-time setup for Minutes — download whisper model, create directories, configure audio input. Use when the user says "set up minutes", "install minutes", "first time setup", "configure minutes", "get started with minutes", "how do I start using minutes", or when verify shows missing components. ([silverstein/minutes](https://github.com/silverstein/minutes/blob/HEAD/.opencode/commands/minutes-setup.md) · ★1.5k · 2 copies)
- **minutes-setup**: Guided first-time setup for Minutes in Jacob's hosted configuration using Deepgram Nova 3 for batch and live transcription, Codex OAuth for summarization, and no local audio or LLM model downloads. ([jacob-split/minutes](https://github.com/jacob-split/minutes/blob/HEAD/.opencode/commands/minutes-setup.md))
- **minutes-tag**: Lightweight outcome tagging for meetings — won, lost, stalled, great, or noise. Use whenever the user says "tag this meeting", "mark that as a win", "that one was a loss", "tag yesterday's call as stalled", "mark this great", "that meeting was noise", "label that meeting", or any time they describe a meeting outcome in passing. Tagging takes 5 seconds and unlocks /minutes-mirror correlation analysis — the more meetings get tagged, the smarter mirror gets at telling the user what behavior patterns lead to wins. Surface this skill any time the user mentions a meeting result, win, loss, or waste… ([silverstein/minutes](https://github.com/silverstein/minutes/blob/HEAD/.opencode/commands/minutes-tag.md) · ★1.5k · 3 copies)
- **minutes-verify**: Verify that Minutes is properly set up and working — model downloaded, mic accessible, directories exist, no stale state. Use when the user says "is minutes working", "check my setup", "verify minutes", "test recording setup", "why isn't minutes working", "minutes health check", or after running setup for the first time. ([silverstein/minutes](https://github.com/silverstein/minutes/blob/HEAD/.opencode/commands/minutes-verify.md) · ★1.5k · 2 copies)
- **minutes-verify**: Verify that Jacob's Minutes install is ready for Deepgram Nova 3 batch and live transcription, Codex summarization command, mic access, directories, and no stale state. ([jacob-split/minutes](https://github.com/jacob-split/minutes/blob/HEAD/.opencode/commands/minutes-verify.md))
- **minutes-video-review**: Analyze a product walkthrough, bug report video, Loom, or ScreenPal using Minutes transcription plus visual review. Use when the user wants a recorded demo or bug clip turned into a durable brief with transcript, key frames, issues, and next steps. ([silverstein/minutes](https://github.com/silverstein/minutes/blob/HEAD/.opencode/commands/minutes-video-review.md) · ★1.5k · 3 copies)

- **minutes-x1-closeout**: Prepare a sourced Minutes meeting outcome for human-governed closeout of existing X1 work. - **mkg-orchestrate**: Memory-grounded multiagent orchestration. Recall prior MKG learnings, deploy subagents to execute a task in verified phases while routing memory into each one, then capture durable new learnings back to the graph. Use to execute, run, or carry out a task or plan with the Meta Knowledge Graph in the loop. ([neo4j-labs/meta-knowledge-graph](https://github.com/neo4j-labs/meta-knowledge-graph/blob/HEAD/commands/mkg-orchestrate.md) · ★25 · 2 copies)
- **mkg-skill-review**: Human-in-the-loop skill publishing for the Meta Knowledge Graph. Walk through the distilled skill proposals waiting in the review queue, validate each against its source learnings, the safety screen, and the live skill it patches, then publish, fix, or discard it through project_resolve_skill. Use when MKG_SKILL_ACTIVATION=human, when session start says skill proposals await review, or when the user asks to review, approve, or publish skills. ([neo4j-labs/meta-knowledge-graph](https://github.com/neo4j-labs/meta-knowledge-graph/blob/HEAD/commands/mkg-skill-review.md) · ★25 · 2 copies)
- **mkg-start**: Onboard a new MKG agent. Check that the meta-knowledge-graph MCP is mounted and which tools are live, inspect the graph state, then either launch the RoadFlex sales demo or build a custom agent persona by capturing user memories. ([neo4j-labs/meta-knowledge-graph](https://github.com/neo4j-labs/meta-knowledge-graph/blob/HEAD/commands/mkg-start.md) · ★25 · 2 copies)

- **module-audit**: Pre-ship module audit pipeline. 7-stage matrix audit covering form components × 4 lenses + 10 cross-cutting axes + adversarial layer + SHA-anchored verification. Use ONLY for module-completion sweeps with ≥3 form components.
